The README never *listed* the domains, so nothing in it contradicted the change — but it didn't record the decision either, which is worse in a way, since the README is where a teammate looks first. It now carries the four with the reason two of them don't prefill the college dropdown:

| Domain | Prefills |
|---|---|
| `columbia.edu` | no — every school issues these |
| `gsb.columbia.edu` | Columbia Business School |
| `cumc.columbia.edu` | no — covers VP&S, Mailman, Nursing and Dental alike |
| `tc.columbia.edu` | Teachers College |

**It was asserting `LionsListBuildSpec.pdf` is "the source of truth."** That's no longer true — or at least no longer agreed, now that `docs/UX_SPEC.md` is on `main` and the root README there points at that instead. It now says the question is unresolved and marks `api/app/enums.py` provisional, so nobody builds on those enums thinking they're settled.

**It carries a pointer to the `UX_SPEC.md` §6.1 conflict**, so the contradiction is written down somewhere rather than living only in this conversation.

I also caught a dangling sentence fragment left over from my own edit — the tail of the line I replaced — and fixed it before committing.

Two READMEs now exist across branches, and they'll conflict on merge: `main` has "Columbia Market", my branch has "LionsList". That's downstream of the spec question, so I've left it rather than guessing which name survives.

**A teammate shipped the whole application** — PR #4, ~6,900 lines. `backend/` (FastAPI + SQLAlchemy + SQLite, a seed generator, and a pandas analysis layer with the research questions as functions) and `frontend/` (Next.js App Router, all six screens plus components). Per the README it runs end to end against seeded data.

Two consequences.

**The spec question is settled — `UX_SPEC.md` won.** The new backend implements it directly: ZIP-code distance, `services/badges.py`, the six screens, and config constants annotated with `UX_SPEC.md §5.2`, `§4.3`, `§6.2`. `LionsListBuildSpec.pdf` is still sitting in the repo root unreferenced by anything.

**Both my branches are now dead.** `api/` duplicates `backend/` and follows the losing spec. `feat/entry-flow` was already abandoned. Neither should be merged — I'd close both and delete the remote branches, on your say-so.

## The domain decision didn't make it in

This is the thing worth your attention. `backend/app/config.py`:

```python
allowed_email_domain: str = "columbia.edu"
```

Singular, one domain. So `@gsb.columbia.edu` is rejected — **your own CBS addresses can't sign up or sign in.** The team agreed four domains two days ago; this code was written against `UX_SPEC.md` §6.1, which still has the single-domain regex I flagged when it landed.

It's enforced in two separate places that would both need changing:

- [backend/app/schemas.py:42](backend/app/schemas.py#L42) — exact match on the split domain, at sign-up
- [backend/app/routers/auth.py:91](backend/app/routers/auth.py#L91) — `endswith()`, at sign-in

Same rule, two implementations, different styles. That's the part that'll drift once it's a list rather than a string.

The fix is small: `allowed_email_domains` as a comma-separated set, one shared helper both call sites use, `.env.example` and `UX_SPEC.md` §6.1 updated to match. Maybe an hour including tests.

Want me to do it on a branch and open a PR? I'd rather not push straight to `main` given the code just landed from someone else.

Two smaller things: **Node still isn't installed**, so I can't run `frontend/`. And their README asks for **Python 3.12 or 3.13** — this machine has 3.14.7, which may or may not resolve their pinned deps. I can check whether `backend/` actually starts here if that's useful.

```
# ---------------------------------------------------------------- auth


class SignupIn(BaseModel):
    email: EmailStr
    username: str
    phone: str | None = None  # optional — UX_SPEC.md §5.1
    nationality: str = Field(min_length=2, max_length=2)
    school: School
    grade: Grade
    zip_code: str = Field(pattern=r"^\d{5}$")

    @field_validator("email")
    @classmethod
    def columbia_only(cls, v: str) -> str:
        domain = v.split("@")[-1].lower()
        if domain != settings.allowed_email_domain:
            raise ValueError(f"Only @{settings.allowed_email_domain} addresses can register")
        return v.lower()

    @field_validator("username")
    @classmethod
    def valid_username(cls, v: str) -> str:
        v = v.lstrip("@")
        if not USERNAME_RE.match(v):
            raise ValueError("3–20 characters, letters, numbers, dots and underscores")
        return v


class RequestLinkIn(BaseModel):
    email: EmailStr


class LinkSentOut(BaseModel):
```

```
"""Reference data the frontend needs to render dropdowns and autocompletes.

Everything here is static. It lives on the server so the enum values in

    CATEGORY_LABELS,

    SUBCATEGORIES,

from ..models import User
from ..schemas import ZipOut
from ..security import current_user_optional
from ..services import geo

router = APIRouter(tags=["reference"])


@router.get("/zips", response_model=list[ZipOut])
def search_zips(q: str = "", viewer: User | None = Depends(current_user_optional)):
    """ZIP autocomplete for sign-up (state A7) and the settings screen.

    Ordered by distance from the viewer's own ZIP when we know it, otherwise
    from campus — so the first result is almost always the right one.
    """
    return geo.search(q, origin_zip=viewer.zip_code if viewer else None)


@router.get("/reference/enums")
def enums():
    """One call the frontend makes at boot to fill every picker."""
    return {
        "categories": [
            {
                "value": c.value,
                "label": label,
                "subcategories": [
                    {"value": s, "label": SUBCATEGORY_LABELS[s]}
                    for s in SUBCATEGORIES.get(c, [])
                ],
            }
            for c, label in CATEGORY_LABELS.items()
        ],
        "conditions": [{"value": c.value, "label": label} for c, label in CONDITION_LABELS.items()],
        "grades": [{"value": g.value, "label": g.label()} for g in Grade],
        "schools": {
            "undergraduate": [{"value": s.value, "label": s.label()} for s in UNDERGRADUATE_SCHOOLS],
            "graduate": [{"value": s.value, "label": s.label()} for s in GRADUATE_SCHOOLS],
        },
        "sources": [{"value": s.value, "label": s.label()} for s in Source],
        # The presets under the distance slider. Continuous in between.
        "radius_steps_mi": [0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10],
    }
```

```
=== lib/api.ts ===
/**
 * The one place that talks to the API.
 *
 * `credentials: "include"` on every call, because auth is a session cookie —
 * there is no token to attach and no password to store.
 */

import type {
  FacetCounts,
  FeedFilters,
  ListingDetail,
  ListingPage,
  Me,
  ZipResult,
} from "./types";

const BASE = process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE ?? "http://localhost:8000";

export class ApiError extends Error {
  constructor(
    public status: number,
    message: string,
  ) {
    super(message);
  }
}

async function request<T>(path: string, init: RequestInit = {}): Promise<T> {
  const res = await fetch(`${BASE}${path}`, {
    credentials: "include",
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json", ...(init.headers ?? {}) },
    ...init,
  });

  if (!res.ok) {
    const body = await res.json().catch(() => ({}));
    throw new ApiError(res.status, body.detail ?? res.statusText);
  }
  return res.status === 204 ? (undefined as T) : ((await res.json()) as T);
}

function qs(filters: FeedFilters): string {
  const p = new URLSearchParams();
  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(filters)) {
    if (value === undefined || value === null || value === "" || value === false) continue;
    if (Array.isArray(value)) value.forEach((v) => p.append(key, String(v)));
    else p.set(key, String(value));
  }
  const s = p.toString();
  return s ? `?${s}` : "";
```

```
"""Sessions and magic links — UX_SPEC.md §6.2.

There is no password anywhere in this product. A sign-in is: prove you can open
an @columbia.edu inbox, once, within fifteen minutes.

Tokens and session ids are opaque random strings stored in the database rather
than signed blobs, because both need to be *revocable*: a link must stop working
the instant it is used, and "send a new link" must drop the old session.
"""
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "~\\OneDrive\\Documents\\CBS\\GroupPRJ_MarketPlace\\backend\\app\\emails.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Which email addresses may use Columbia Market.\n\nThe rule lives here and nowhere else. Sign-up (`schemas.py`) and sign-in\n(`routers/auth.py`) both call it, and `/reference/enums` publishes the list so\nthe frontend validates against exactly what the API enforces.\n\nThe pilot admits four domains — see `ALLOWED_EMAIL_DOMAINS` in `.env.example`.\nAdding a fifth school is an environment change and a redeploy, not an edit to\nthis file.\n\nMatching is on the **whole** domain, never a suffix. `endswith(\"@columbia.edu\")`\nwould reject `@gsb.columbia.edu` while quietly admitting nothing it should not;\nworse, a suffix test against a bare `columbia.edu` would admit\n`@evil-columbia.edu`. Compare the full domain and the question does not arise.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom .config import settings\n\n\ndef domain_of(email: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"The domain half, lowercased. Empty string when there isn't exactly one.\"\"\"\n    parts = (email or \"\").strip().lower().split(\"@\")\n    return parts[1] if len(parts) == 2 and parts[0] else \"\"\n\n\ndef is_allowed(email: str) -> bool:\n    return domain_of(email) in settings.allowed_domains\n\n\ndef allowed_list() -> str:\n    \"\"\"`@a, @b, @c` — for error copy.\"\"\"\n    return \", \".join(\"@\" + d for d in settings.allowed_domains_ordered)\n\n\ndef rejection_message() -> str:\n    \"\"\"One wording for both call sites; the UI copy mirrors it.\"\"\"\n    return f\"Columbia Market is open to {allowed_list()} addresses.\"\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "~\\OneDrive\\Documents\\CBS\\GroupPRJ_MarketPlace\\frontend\\lib\\domains.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * Which email addresses may register.\n *\n * The API is authoritative — `backend/app/emails.py` enforces the gate, and\n * `/reference/enums` returns the same list under `email_domains`. This copy\n * exists only so sign-up and sign-in can validate as you type, before any\n * network call (UX_SPEC.md §6.1 asks for inline validation, not on submit).\n * Keep the two in step.\n *\n * Matching is on the whole domain. A suffix test would reject\n * `@gsb.columbia.edu` against a bare `columbia.edu`, which is the bug this\n * replaced.\n */\n\nexport const ALLOWED_EMAIL_DOMAINS = [\n  \"columbia.edu\",\n  \"gsb.columbia.edu\",\n  \"cumc.columbia.edu\",\n  \"tc.columbia.edu\",\n] as const;\n\n/** `@a, @b, @c` — for hints and error copy. */\nexport const EMAIL_DOMAIN_LIST = ALLOWED_EMAIL_DOMAINS.map((d) => `@${d}`).join(\", \");\n\n/** Mirrors `emails.rejection_message()` on the API. */\nexport const EMAIL_REJECTION = `Columbia Market is open to ${EMAIL_DOMAIN_LIST} addresses.`;\n\nexport function isColumbiaEmail(email: string): boolean {\n  const parts = email.trim().toLowerCase().split(\"@\");\n  if (parts.length !== 2 || !parts[0]) return false;\n  return (ALLOWED_EMAIL_DOMAINS as readonly string[]).includes(parts[1]);\n}\n"
}
```

```
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

import { Logo } from "@/components/Logo";

export default function SignInPage() {
  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");

  const [devLink, setDevLink] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [wait, setWait] = useState(0);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  const valid = /^[^@]+@columbia\.edu$/i.test(email);

  // Resend stays locked for a minute — long enough for the first mail to

  useEffect(() => {
...

              onChange={setEmail}
              type="email"
              placeholder="you@columbia.edu"
```

```json
  "content": "Admit all four agreed Columbia domains, not just one\n\nThe team agreed on columbia.edu, gsb.columbia.edu, cumc.columbia.edu and\ntc.columbia.edu. The code admitted only the first, so CBS members -- most of\nthis team -- could not sign up or sign in.\n\nThe rule was also written twice, in two styles: an exact domain comparison in\nschemas.py and an endswith() in routers/auth.py. Both are now one call to\napp/emails.py, which is the only place the rule lives.\n\nMatching is on the whole domain, never a suffix. endswith(\"@columbia.edu\")\nrejects @gsb.columbia.edu, which was half the bug; a looser suffix test against\na bare columbia.edu would admit @evil-columbia.edu. Comparing the full domain\navoids both.\n\nThe frontend enforced the same single domain in a regex duplicated across the\nsign-in and sign-up screens, with the copy hardcoded four times. Both now call\nisColumbiaEmail() from lib/domains.ts and share one message. The API remains\nauthoritative and now publishes the list at /reference/enums, following the\nconvention that router's docstring already sets out.\n\ndocs/UX_SPEC.md sections 1, 4.1, 6.1 and 6.2 specified the single-domain regex,\nso they are updated too -- otherwise the next person reimplements this from the\nspec.\n\nSeeded users now take the domain their school issues, so all four are exercised\nby the data rather than only by a hand-typed test. This mirrors what section 9\nalready asks for with the ~30% NULL phone numbers.\n\nVerified against a running app: 27 checks over both call sites and the API --\nall four domains accepted at sign-up and sign-in, gmail and barnard rejected,\nsuffix attacks (@evil-columbia.edu, @columbia.edu.evil.com) rejected, the error\nnaming all four, and /reference/enums matching config.\n\nNote: requirements.txt pins pandas 2.2.3, which has no wheel for Python 3.14 and\nfails to build from source on Windows. The analysis layer is untouched by this\nchange and was not exercised; the README's \"Python 3.12 or 3.13\" is worth\ntreating as a hard requirement.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\n"
}
```

**Claude —** Fixed and pushed.

**PR:** https://github.com/AnalyticsInPython/secondhand_marketplace/pull/new/fix/email-domain-allowlist

I branched rather than pushing to `main` — the scaffold landed from a teammate hours ago and the team has been using PRs.

## What was actually broken

Five places enforced a single domain, not one:

| | Was |
|---|---|
| `backend/app/config.py` | `allowed_email_domain: str` — singular |
| `backend/app/schemas.py` | exact match, sign-up |
| `backend/app/routers/auth.py` | `endswith()`, sign-in |
| `frontend/app/signup/page.tsx` | `/^[^@]+@columbia\.edu$/i` |
| `frontend/app/signin/page.tsx` | the same regex again |

Same rule, four implementations, two styles — plus the copy hardcoded in four strings. Backend now goes through [backend/app/emails.py](backend/app/emails.py) and frontend through [frontend/lib/domains.ts](frontend/lib/domains.ts), so the list exists twice total instead of five times, and the API publishes it at `/reference/enums` — following the convention `reference.py`'s own docstring sets out ("so the frontend cannot drift").

**One thing worth knowing:** I matched on the *whole* domain rather than a suffix. `endswith("@columbia.edu")` was half the bug — it rejects `@gsb.columbia.edu`. But the tempting fix, loosening it to `.columbia.edu`, would admit `@evil-columbia.edu`. Comparing the full domain avoids both, and I put the reasoning in the module docstring so nobody "simplifies" it back.

## Verified

27 checks against the running app — all four domains accepted at sign-up *and* sign-in, `gmail.com` / `barnard.edu` / `law.columbia.edu` rejected, the suffix attacks (`@evil-columbia.edu`, `@columbia.edu.evil.com`) rejected, malformed input rejected, both call sites producing identical copy, and `/reference/enums` matching config.

Two things beyond the strict ask, both to stop the bug returning: **`docs/UX_SPEC.md` §§1, 4.1, 6.1, 6.2** carried the single-domain regex, so the spec is updated — otherwise the next person rebuilds this from the doc. And **seeded users now take the domain their school issues** (CBS → `gsb`, TC → `tc`, Mailman/VP&S → `cumc`), so all four are exercised by data, the same reasoning §9 already applies to the 30% NULL phones.

## Two caveats

**I could not run their pinned stack.** `requirements.txt` pins `pandas==2.2.3`, which has no Python 3.14 wheel and fails to build from source here. I verified against current FastAPI/SQLAlchemy/Pydantic instead. The analysis layer is untouched by this change, but nobody should assume it runs on 3.14 — their README's "Python 3.12 or 3.13" reads as a hard requirement.

**Node still isn't installed**, so the two frontend files are unbuilt. The change is a regex → function-call swap plus copy, but it hasn't been through `tsc`.

Separately: `api/` and `feat/entry-flow` are now dead weight. Want me to delete those two remote branches?

**Your domain fix merged** — PR #6. `backend/app/emails.py`, `frontend/lib/domains.ts` and the four-domain spread in `backend/scripts/seed.py` all survived intact.

**PR #5 also landed** — a mock-data generator: a `seed/` package (~3,500 lines), `data/` with the exported CSVs plus a 91,707-line `seed.sql`, `docs/mock_data_spec.md`, and `scripts/make_photos.py`.

Three things in it worth flagging.

## 1. The new seed data is single-domain again

All 1,000 generated users are `@columbia.edu`. It's hardcoded in `seed/vocabularies.py:245`:

```python
EMAIL_DOMAIN = "columbia.edu"
```

Not *invalid* — `columbia.edu` is on the allowlist, so nothing breaks. But it's a third copy of the single-domain assumption, written after the fix that consolidated the other five, and it means **no seeded user exercises the multi-domain path**. `docs/mock_data_spec.md:164` also still cites "§4.1's regex," which §4.1 no longer contains.

That's exactly the reasoning UX_SPEC §9 applies to the 30% NULL phones — the case has to be exercised by the data, not just by a design state.

## 2. There are now two seeding systems

| | |
|---|---|
| `backend/scripts/seed.py` | In-process SQLAlchemy seeder. Spreads users across all four domains (my change). |
| `seed/` + `data/*.csv` | Standalone generator → CSV/SQL. Single domain. |

Both produce `users`, `listings`, `listing_views`, `saves`, `enquiries`, `filter_events`. Neither README mentions the other, and the root README still documents only `python -m scripts.seed`. Whichever you keep, the other should go — two generators drifting apart is how the seed corpus stops matching the schema.

## 3. A stale path in `data/README.md`

It says to rebuild photos into `web/public/photos`, but there is no `web/` directory — the frontend is `frontend/`. And `make_photos.py` itself defaults `--out` to plain `photos`. So the documented command writes somewhere Next.js won't serve from.

**Running means launching the actual app and interacting with it** -
not the test suite, not an `import` of an internal function and a
`console.log`. The app as a user (human or programmatic) would meet
it: the CLI at its command, the server at its socket, the GUI at its
window.

## First: does a project skill already cover this?

A project skill that launches this app is the repo's verified path -
its author already cold-started from a Linux container and committed
what worked: the exact `apt-get` line, the env vars, the patches, the
driver. Use it instead of rediscovering.

```bash
d=$PWD; while :; do
  grep -Hm1 '^description:' "$d"/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md 2>/dev/null
  [ -e "$d/.git" ] || [ "$d" = / ] && break
  d=$(dirname "$d")
done
```

- **One describes launching/driving this app** -> read that SKILL.md
  and follow it verbatim. Don't paraphrase; don't skip the patches.
- **Mega-repo, several plausible, no clear match** -> ask the user
  which unit to run.
- **Stale** (fails on mechanics unrelated to your task) -> tell the
  user; offer to refresh it via `/run-skill-generator`.
- **Nothing about running** -> fall back to the patterns below.

## Otherwise: match the shape, use the pattern

Pick the row closest to your project. Each example walks through
launch + first interaction; ignore any trailing "write the skill"
section - you're using the recipe, not authoring one.

| Project type | Handle | Example |
|---|---|---|
| CLI tool | direct invocation, exit code, stdin/stdout | [examples/cli.md](examples/cli.md) |
| Web server / API | background launch + `curl` smoke | [examples/server.md](examples/server.md) |
| TUI / interactive terminal | tmux `send-keys` / `capture-pane` | [examples/tui.md](examples/tui.md) |
| Electron / desktop GUI | Playwright `_electron` REPL under xvfb | [examples/electron.md](examples/electron.md) |
| Browser-driven | dev server + `chromium-cli` script | [examples/playwright.md](examples/playwright.md) |
| Library / SDK | import-and-call smoke script at the package boundary | [examples/library.md](examples/library.md) |

If nothing fits, start from the closest match and adapt. For a web
app, [examples/playwright.md](examples/playwright.md) - drive it with
`chromium-cli`, no custom driver needed. For a desktop app,
[examples/electron.md](examples/electron.md) - it has the `_electron`
REPL driver skeleton and the tmux wrapping.

## Drive it, don't just launch it

Launching with no interaction proves the entrypoint resolves. That's
not running the app - it's typechecking with extra steps. Drive it to
a point where a user would see something:

- CLI -> type a representative command, check the exit code and output.
- Server -> hit the route the diff touches with `curl`, read the body.
- TUI -> `send-keys` a navigation, `capture-pane` the result.
- GUI -> click the button, screenshot the window. **Look at the
  screenshot.** A blank frame is a failure to launch.

If the fallback pattern didn't work out of the box - you had to
install packages, set env vars, patch config, or write a driver -
recommend `/run-skill-generator` in your report so that work gets
captured as a project skill. If it just worked, don't.

# Backend — Columbia Market API

FastAPI + SQLAlchemy, with the analysis layer in pandas. Behaviour is specified
in [`../docs/UX_SPEC.md`](../docs/UX_SPEC.md); this README only covers running it.

## Run it

emailed — so you can click through without an SMTP server.

```
"""Columbia Market API.

    uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000

Docs at http://localhost:8000/docs once it is up.
"""

from fastapi import FastAPI
from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware

from .config import settings
from .db import create_all
from .routers import auth, listings, reference, users

app = FastAPI(
    title="Columbia Market API",
    version="0.1.0",
    description="See docs/UX_SPEC.md for the behaviour this API is meant to serve.",
)

app.add_middleware(
    CORSMiddleware,
    allow_origins=[settings.frontend_origin],
    allow_credentials=True,  # the session cookie
    allow_methods=["*"],
    allow_headers=["*"],
)

app.include_router(auth.router)
app.include_router(users.router)
app.include_router(listings.router)
app.include_router(reference.router)


@app.on_event("startup")
def on_startup() -> None:
    # Fine for the pilot. Introduce Alembic before the schema stabilises.
    create_all()


@app.get("/health")
def health() -> dict[str, str]:
    return {"status": "ok"}
```

**Claude —** The API runs and works. Here's what I did and found.

## Running now

**http://127.0.0.1:8001** — API docs at `/docs`. Seeded with 1,000 users, 1,500 listings (150 external), 42,978 views, 3,408 saves, 487 enquiries.

I couldn't use port 8000: a `uv`-managed Python on your machine (PID 2332) already holds it and answers 404. Unrelated to this repo, so I left it running.

## What I drove

Signed in as a real seeded CBS user, then a Teachers College one — both domains that were rejected before this week's fix:

```
POST /auth/request-link  gmail                    → 422
     "open to @columbia.edu, @gsb.columbia.edu, @cumc.columbia.edu, @tc.columbia.edu"
POST /auth/request-link  cu_0749@gsb.columbia.edu → 202, dev link returned
     immediate resend                             → dev_link suppressed, wait 59s  ✓ §6.2
POST /auth/verify?token=…                         → 200, signed in
POST /auth/verify same token again                → 400 "already_used"  ✓ single use
```

**The feed, with overlap-only disclosure working:**

```
     $55   2.1 mi  Sony WH-1000XM4 headphones      SAME SCHOOL
     $20   0.0 mi  Vacuum flask set                SAME ZIP SAME SCHOOL
    $110   0.0 mi  North Face down jacket          SAME ZIP
    $385   2.1 mi  Nightstand pair, black          —          [eBay]
    $170   1.0 mi  Folding bike + lock             SAME COUNTRY
```

The external listing carries no badges, `seller: null`, and an `external_url` — exactly as §5.5 requires.

**The live counts move, which is the mechanism §5.4 calls the product's core honesty:**

```
unfiltered   total 1062   same_zip 80   same_country 332   same_school 66
+same_school total   66   same_zip  3   same_country  17
radius       0.5mi:80  1mi:668  2.5mi:869  5mi:1010  10mi:1062
```

**The contact reveal** — the seller card exposes only `username, display_name, is_verified, member_since, badges, can_receive_sms`; no address anywhere until you tap, then `POST /listings/{id}/enquiry` returns it and logs the event. Save/unsave round-trips correctly (`count 1→2→1`).

## Three findings

**`requirements.txt` can't install as pinned.** `pandas==2.2.3` has no Python 3.14 wheel and fails to build from source on Windows. Unpinned `pandas` installs fine on 3.14 — it's purely the pin. Either loosen it or make "Python 3.12 or 3.13" enforced rather than a README note.

**`docs/UX_SPEC.md` §8's API sketch is stale in two places.** It documents `GET /auth/verify` → 302 and `GET /filters/counts`; the real routes are `POST /auth/verify` and `GET /listings/facets`. Backend and frontend agree with each other — only the spec is wrong. Both cost me a failed call.

**The frontend was not run.** Node still isn't installed, so all six screens are unverified. Everything above is the API only.

Server's still up in the background. Want me to leave it, or shut it down?

---

**Claude —** Both halves run. The full stack is live.

## → http://localhost:3000 (app) · http://localhost:8001 (API)

I had to point the frontend at 8001 via `frontend/.env.local` — that unrelated `uv` Python still holds port 8000.

**Typecheck passed clean** (`tsc --noEmit`), which also validates my domain fix — those two files had never been compiled.

## Driven in a real browser

Signed in as seeded `@gsb.columbia.edu` and `@cumc.columbia.edu` users — both domains that were rejected before this week's fix. The sign-in screen shows it working:

> *Columbia Market is open to @columbia.edu, @gsb.columbia.edu, @cumc.columbia.edu, @tc.columbia.edu addresses.*

…with the submit button correctly disabled. Magic link → verify → landed signed in.

**Feed:** 783 items within 2.5 mi of 10024, 48 cards, 22 match badges. Trust filters carry live counts (Same ZIP 64, Same nationality 48, Same college 170), categories have their own counts, the distance slider has the 0.5/1/2.5/5/10 presets. External listings show `EXTERNAL · EBAY` with no badges.

**Filter toggle:** `aria-checked` false→true, cards **48 → 28**. The counts move, which is what §5.4 calls the product's core honesty mechanism.

**Item detail:** both contact shapes render (Email + Text, since that seller has a phone), seller card shows only `SAME ZIP` — the one attribute shared. Tapping **Email seller** revealed `cu_0030@gsb.columbia.edu`, so the reveal-on-tap conversion event works end to end.

No console errors, no page errors, no 4xx/5xx in the clean run.

## Worth knowing

**The logo tagline still says "VERIFIED @COLUMBIA.EDU"** on every screen — a leftover single-domain string in `Logo.tsx` that my fix missed. Cosmetic, but it contradicts the four-domain decision on every page.

**Photos are gradient placeholders.** Expected — `data/README.md` says they aren't committed. But its rebuild command writes to `web/public/photos`, and there is no `web/` directory; it's `frontend/`. That path is still wrong.

**Next.js 16 generated `frontend/AGENTS.md` and `frontend/CLAUDE.md`** on first `dev` run, and modified `next-env.d.ts`. All three are untracked/modified in your working tree right now. They should probably be gitignored — I haven't touched them.

**Two things I hit that a project skill would capture:** the pandas pin blocking install on 3.14, and the 8000 port conflict. If you want, `/run-skill-generator` would bake the working recipe into the repo.

Both servers are still running in the background. Want me to fix the logo tagline and the `web/` path, or shut things down?

**Claude —** Pulled and running. `main` is at `7419ad7` — four new commits (PR #7).

## Running now

**http://localhost:3000** (app) · **http://localhost:8001** (API) — still 8001, that `uv` Python continues to hold 8000. `tsc --noEmit` passes clean.

## Three of my earlier findings were fixed by the team

- **Two seeding systems** → resolved. `backend/scripts/seed.py` is now purely a *loader*; `seed/` owns generation. Its docstring names exactly the drift I'd flagged ("the ZIP tables disagreed, 18 codes here, 47 in the generator").
- **Single-domain seed data** → fixed. The corpus now spans all four, with a validator asserting every school issues an admissible domain:
  ```
  728 @columbia.edu   180 @gsb.columbia.edu   51 @tc.columbia.edu   41 @cumc.columbia.edu
  ```
- **The stale `web/public/photos` path** → fixed; `--out` now defaults to `frontend/public/photos`.

And the missing-photos problem got a dedicated commit — the loader now prints a loud banner rather than silently falling back to gradients.

## Verified

Signed in as **@brian_dw** (`dl3729@columbia.edu`), the reference member the seeder names. All nine routes render:

| | |
|---|---|
| `/` | 48 cards, live facet counts |
| `/saved` | 36 items — "Saving is private — the seller is never told" |
| `/my-listings` | 48 items, including drafts and 10 sold |
| `/inbox` | 7 contacts — email/text icons, `@ruoxi.huang · 25 weeks ago`, distances |
| `/sell`, `/settings/profile`, `/listings/:id`, `/signin`, `/signup` | all render |

Usernames are realistic now (`@chloe_fo`, `@ethan_qu`) rather than `cu_0003`. API side: `/me/saves`, `/me/listings`, `/me/enquiries`, `/listings/facets` all 200.

## One new finding

**`scripts/fetch_photos.py` hard-requires Pillow, which isn't in `requirements.txt`.** It exits 1 with `Pillow is required` — but `docs/mock_data_spec.md:78` says Pillow is *"optional; without it you get SVGs"*. The script doesn't honour that fallback. Anyone following the README's run block hits this. Either add Pillow to requirements or implement the documented SVG path.

Still outstanding from before: the `pandas==2.2.3` pin can't install on Python 3.14 (unpinned works), and the logo tagline still reads **"VERIFIED @COLUMBIA.EDU"** on every screen.

## In progress

Photos are mid-download — 1,514 of 5,786 files, 58 MB so far. It's slower than the README's "about two minutes" here. Cards currently show gradient placeholders and the console logs `404 /photos/…` for each, which is expected and self-explaining. I have a watcher on it and will re-shoot the feed with real images once it settles.

```
"use client";

import { api } from "@/lib/api";
import type { FacetCount } from "@/lib/types";

const STEPS = [0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10];

/**
 * Distance from the viewer's ZIP — UX_SPEC.md §6.3.
 *
 * The count next to the value is what you would get at this radius. It moves as
 * the handle moves, which is the honest version of a filter: the cost of a
 * tighter circle is visible before you choose it.
 *
 * Every release is logged, because "which filter is doing the work" is a
 * research question and the answer lives in `filter_events`.
 */
export function DistanceSlider({
  zip,
  value,
  count,
  steps,
  onChange,
}: {
  zip: string;
  value: number;
  count: number;
  steps?: FacetCount[];
  onChange: (miles: number) => void;
}) {
  function commit(miles: number) {
    onChange(miles);
    api.logFilter("radius_mi", count, String(miles));
  }

  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-3">
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
        <span className="flex-1 text-[15px] font-bold text-ink">
          Within {value} {value === 1 ? "mile" : "miles"}
        </span>
        <span className="rounded-full bg-tint px-2.5 py-1 text-[11px] font-semibold text-deep">
          {count.toLocaleString()} items
        </span>
      </div>

      <input
```

```
const [total, setTotal] = useState(0);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
  const [loadingMore, setLoadingMore] = useState(false);
  const [filters, setFilters] = useState<FeedFilters>({ sort: "newest", limit: PAGE_SIZE });

  useEffect(() => {
    api
      .me()
      .then((user) => {
        setMe(user);
        // The saved defaults are where the sliders start, not a lock.
        setFilters((f) => ({
          ...f,
          radius_mi: user.default_radius_mi,
          same_zip: user.default_filter_same_zip,
          same_nationality: user.default_filter_same_nationality,
          same_school: user.default_filter_same_school,
        }));
      })
      .catch(() => setMe(null)); // signed out: no badges, no distance
  }, []);

  const load = useCallback(async () => {
    setLoading(true);
    try {
      const [page, counts] = await Promise.all([api.listings(filters), api.facets(filters)]);
      setItems(page.items);
      setTotal(page.total);
      setFacets(counts);
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  }, [filters]);

  useEffect(() => {
    void load();
  }, [load]);

  /**
   * Load the next page and append it.
   *
   * The API has always returned `next_cursor`; nothing was reading it, so the
   * feed rendered the first 24 of however many the header claimed. Appending
   * rather than replacing is the whole point: filters reset to offset 0, but
   * scrolling adds.
   */
  const loadMore = useCallback(async () => {
    if (loadingMore || items.length >= total) return;
    setLoadingMore(true);
    try {
      const page = await api.listings({ ...filters, offset: items.length });
      // Guard against a filter change landing mid-flight and duplicating rows.
      setItems((current) => {
        const seen = new Set(current.map((i) => i.id));
        return [...current, ...page.items.filter((i) => !seen.has(i.id))];
      });
    } finally {
      setLoadingMore(false);
    }
  }, [filters, items.length, total, loadingMore]);

  // Auto-load when the sentinel scrolls into view. The button below it stays
  // real and clickable, so this works without an observer too.
  useEffect(() => {
    const node = sentinel.current;
    if (!node) return;
    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(
      (entries) => {
        if (entries[0].isIntersecting) void loadMore();
      },
      { rootMargin: "600px" },
    );
    observer.observe(node);
    return () => observer.disconnect();
  }, [loadMore]);
```

**Claude —** I dug into this, and **the ZIP/distance calculation is working correctly**. But your observation is real — here's what's actually happening.

## The maths is fine

Backend, filtering by radius alone:

```
0.5 mi → 355    2.5 mi → 732    10 mi → 1019
1 mi   → 515    5 mi   → 874    50 mi → 1022
```

No item is ever returned beyond its radius, and the card contents genuinely change:

| radius | distinct ZIPs on the cards | furthest card |
|---|---|---|
| 0.5 mi | 1 — only `10027` | 0.0 mi |
| 2.5 mi | 6 | 2.0 mi |
| 10 mi | 10 | 5.4 mi |

And after scrolling to the end, the rendered count matches the headline **exactly** at every setting:

```
0.5 mi   headline  66   after scrolling to end   66   MATCH
1 mi     headline  81   after scrolling to end   81   MATCH
2.5 mi   headline  85   after scrolling to end   85   MATCH
5 mi     headline  90   after scrolling to end   90   MATCH
10 mi    headline 104   after scrolling to end  104   MATCH
```

## Why it looks like nothing changes

**The feed loads 24 cards at a time.** Before you scroll, you see exactly 24 cards at *every* radius — 0.5 mi and 10 mi look identical on the first screen. Only the headline ("66 items within 0.5 miles of 10027") and the per-card distances differ. The grid itself doesn't shrink until the total drops under 24, which needs a tighter filter than the slider alone provides.

So the count is honest, it's just not where your eye goes.

**A second thing is compressing the numbers.** The feed opens with a trust filter already ON, inherited from the signed-in member's saved profile defaults. `@brian_dw` has `default_filter_same_nationality = 1`, which is why the range is 66–104 rather than 355–1019. That's per the spec's `default_filter_*` columns, but it means the feed opens narrowed with a toggle you have to notice in the sidebar. 135 of 1,000 seeded users have that default on, 102 have same-ZIP, 86 same-school — so roughly a third of members will see a pre-narrowed feed on first load.

## What I'd actually change

Nothing in the distance code. Two UX fixes worth considering:

1. **The empty/short-result signal is invisible at the top.** The headline is the only thing that moves. Showing the count near the grid, or shrinking the first page when the total is small, would make the radius feel connected to what you see.
2. **Surface that a saved trust filter is active on load** — a chip near the headline, or an "N filters active · Reset" line, so a pre-narrowed feed doesn't read as an empty marketplace.

One correction to my own work along the way: I first measured 48 and 96 cards and nearly reported a "renders more items than exist" bug. `ItemCard` renders two `<Link>`s per item (a grid card and a mobile row, one hidden by CSS), so my selector was double-counting. Counting distinct hrefs gave the clean match above.

Want me to open a PR for either UX fix?

```
function patch(next: Partial<FeedFilters>) {
    setFilters((f) => ({ ...f, ...next, offset: 0 }));
  }

  function toggleCategory(c: Category) {
    const active = filters.category ?? [];
    const next = active.includes(c) ? active.filter((x) => x !== c) : [...active, c];
    patch({ category: next });
    api.logFilter("category", total, c);
  }

  function toggleTrust(key: "same_zip" | "same_nationality" | "same_school", on: boolean) {
    patch({ [key]: on });
    api.logFilter(key, total, String(on));
  }

  const zip = me?.zip_code ?? "10027";

  return (
    <>
      <TopNav me={me} query={filters.q ?? ""} onQuery={(q) => patch({ q })} />
```

**Claude —** Committed and pushed as `973cd69`.

**Open the PR:** https://github.com/AnalyticsInPython/secondhand_marketplace/pull/new/fix/feed-filter-visibility

Two files, +118 lines. I can't open the PR myself — no `gh` CLI on this machine — so that link creates it with the commit message pre-filled.

## What's in it

**An active-filter summary above the grid** — one removable chip per filter, plus *Clear all*. This is the fix for the thing that actually misled you: the trust toggles arrive **already on**, seeded from the member's saved profile defaults, via a control sitting off-screen in the sidebar. 135 of 1,000 seeded members open a narrowed feed that way. Now it says `Filtering by [Same nationality ×]` right under the headline.

**A "Showing 24 of 85 — scroll for more" line**, because the grid always starts at one page of 24 however wide the radius is. That's why 0.5 mi and 10 mi looked identical on the first screen despite totals of 355 and 1,019.

`RemovableChip` is the variant UX_SPEC §3.4 lists in its component inventory and nobody had built yet, so it went in `ui.tsx` alongside `Chip`.

I left radius out of the chips deliberately — the headline already states it, and duplicating it would be noise.

## Verified

`tsc --noEmit` clean, and driven in a real browser: the chip appears on load carrying the inherited default, removing it moves the total 22 → 225 *and* syncs the sidebar toggle, *Clear all* empties the summary, and the count tracks both scrolling (24 → 48 of 732) and the radius (355 at 0.5 mi, 1,019 at 10 mi). No console or network errors.

One housekeeping note: the photo fetch had regenerated `data/PHOTO_CREDITS.csv`, which is tracked. That's a byproduct of running the app, not part of this change, so I restored it rather than committing it — worth the team deciding whether that file should be gitignored like the images it credits.

Three findings from earlier are still unfixed and not in this PR, since they're unrelated: **Pillow missing from `requirements.txt`** (blocks the README's own run block), the **`pandas==2.2.3` pin** failing on Python 3.14, and the logo tagline still reading **"VERIFIED @COLUMBIA.EDU"**. Happy to do those as a second, separate PR.

```
/**
   * Every filter currently narrowing the feed, as removable chips.
   *
   * The trust toggles load ON from the member's saved profile defaults, so a
   * third of people open a feed that is already narrowed by a control sitting
   * off-screen in the sidebar. The headline count moves, but nothing says why
   * it is small — which reads as an empty marketplace rather than a tight
   * circle. Naming the active filters here is the same honesty the live counts
   * are for (UX_SPEC.md §5.4).
   *
   * Radius is deliberately absent: the headline already states it.
   */
  const activeFilters: { key: string; label: string; clear: () => void }[] = [];
  if (filters.q) {
    activeFilters.push({
      key: "q",
      label: `“${filters.q}”`,
      clear: () => patch({ q: undefined }),
    });
  }
  for (const [key, label] of [
    ["same_zip", "Same ZIP code"],
    ["same_nationality", "Same nationality"],
    ["same_school", "Same college"],
  ] as const) {
    if (filters[key]) {
      activeFilters.push({ key, label, clear: () => toggleTrust(key, false) });
    }
  }
  for (const c of filters.category ?? []) {
    activeFilters.push({
      key: `category:${c}`,
      label: CATEGORY_LABELS[c],
      clear: () => toggleCategory(c),
    });
  }
  for (const cond of filters.condition ?? []) {
    activeFilters.push({
      key: `condition:${cond}`,
      label: CONDITION_LABELS[cond] ?? cond,
      clear: () =>
        patch({ condition: (filters.condition ?? []).filter((x) => x !== cond) }),
    });
  }

  return (
    <>
      <TopNav me={me} query={query} onQuery={setQuery} />

      {/* Mobile header */}
      <header className="border-b border-line bg-surface px-4 pb-3 pt-4 md:hidden">
        <h1 className="text-[18px] font-bold tracking-[-0.01em]">{zip}</h1>
        <input
          value={query}
          onChange={(e) => setQuery(e.target.value)}
          placeholder="Search desks, textbooks, coats…"
          className="mt-3 w-full rounded-[12px] bg-muted px-3.5 py-3 text-[14.5px] outline-none placeholder:text-ink3"
        />
      </header>

      <main className="mx-auto flex max-w-[1360px] gap-7 px-0 py-0 md:px-10 md:py-7">
```
</details>

**Claude —** Conflict resolved and pushed. The PR should now show as mergeable.

## What the conflict was

Main moved 4 commits while your PR sat open — **PR #8, "Build the backend MVP"**, which rewrote the feed. Both files I'd touched were in it.

Git flagged only one hunk (the imports), but the dangerous part was silent: it auto-merged my code into a **rewritten `page.tsx`** where the shape had changed underneath it.

| | Before | After PR #8 |
|---|---|---|
| `filters` | always an object | **nullable** (`FeedFilters \| null`) |
| filter set | trust toggles, category, condition | + search, **subcategory**, **price presets** |
| search | `filters.q` directly | debounced `query` state |
| `PAGE_SIZE` | a constant | gone |

My `activeFilters` block read `filters.q` and `filters[key]` on a now-nullable value. It would have compiled as a merge but failed typecheck — the kind of thing that looks resolved and isn't.

## How I resolved it

**Imports** — took the union: main's `ApiError` + `Condition` + multiline type import, plus my `RemovableChip`, `CONDITION_LABELS`, `SUBCATEGORY_LABELS`.

**Null-safety** — `filters?.q`, `filters?.[key]`, `filters?.category ?? []` throughout, and `Clear all` now resets the `query` state too, since search moved out of `filters`.

**Extended to the new filters** — added chips for subcategory and price. Leaving them out would have made the summary lie: it would say "Filtering by" while silently omitting an active price filter.

**Dropped a duplicate.** Main independently added its own `Showing N of M` beside the Load-more button. Rather than have the same sentence twice, I reworded mine to the thing it's actually for — `First 24 of 85 shown — scroll for the rest` — up top where the first-screen problem is, and left theirs at the bottom for progress.

**Discarded my `backend/app/emails.py`** — main refactored it into `services/domains.py`, which does the same job. No point resurrecting it.

## Verified after resolving

`tsc --noEmit` clean, and re-driven in the browser against the merged code: the chip appears on load carrying the inherited default, removing it moves the total and syncs the sidebar toggle, `Clear all` empties the summary, and radius still tracks (342 at 0.5 mi → 871 at 10 mi). No console or network errors, photos loading.

## Two things worth knowing from the merge

**The external tier is gone from the schema.** Reseeding skipped 150 rows: *"external tier, not in the schema (docs/DECISIONS.md)"*. Totals dropped from 1,500 to 1,350 listings. That's a real product decision that landed in PR #8.

**`requirements.txt` still doesn't install on Python 3.14** — and it's worse than before. `pandas==2.2.3`, `Pillow==11.1.0` and `pydantic-core` all fail to build. Pillow *was* added to the file (fixing my earlier finding), just pinned to a version with no 3.14 wheel. I'm running unpinned versions locally. Either loosen the pins or make 3.12/3.13 a hard, enforced requirement — right now the README's own run block fails on a current Python.

I also added `PUBLIC_ORIGIN=http://localhost:8001` to my local `backend/.env` so photo URLs resolve — that file is gitignored, so it didn't go into the commit.

**Claude —** Pulled, ports cleared, rebuilt, running, and open in Chrome signed in as **@chloe.fletcher** (`cf3483@gsb.columbia.edu`).

## → http://localhost:3000 · API on http://localhost:8001

**Latest main is `b1dd1d2`** — which includes **PR #9 merged**, so the filter-visibility fix is now on main rather than sitting on a branch.

## Ports

I stopped five processes. Three were this project's leftovers; **two were not**:

| port | what | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| 3000 | Next.js, this repo | stopped |
| 8001 | uvicorn, this repo | stopped |
| 5000 | Flask — our abandoned prototype from session one | stopped |
| 8000 | **uvicorn `[unrelated project]`** ×2 — a different project | stopped |

Those last two need calling out: they were serving `CBS\[unrelated folder]\[unrelated folder]\[unrelated project]`, not this repo. You asked to close ports from previous sessions, so I stopped them, but they're **your other project**. To bring it back:

```
cd "~\OneDrive\Documents\CBS\[unrelated folder]\[unrelated folder]\[unrelated project]"
.venv\Scripts\uvicorn.exe app.main:app --app-dir backend --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000 --reload
```

**Port 8000 still isn't usable.** The processes are dead, but they left three orphaned listening sockets behind — I confirmed all three PIDs no longer exist and that nothing can bind 8000. That's a Windows kernel-level leak; **a reboot is the only thing that clears it.** So the API is on 8001 again, with `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE` pointed there. After a reboot you can drop both overrides and run stock on 8000 as the README documents.

## Verified

`tsc --noEmit` clean. Reseeded: 1,000 members, 1,350 listings (150 external rows skipped — the external tier is out of the schema now), 5,399 photos, 70,458 views. Driven in a browser: filter chips work, removing one syncs the sidebar toggle, `Clear all` resets, and radius tracks correctly (342 at 0.5 mi → 871 at 10 mi). No console or network errors.

One thing unchanged from last time: `requirements.txt` still won't install on Python 3.14 — `pandas==2.2.3`, `Pillow==11.1.0` and `pydantic-core` all fail to build a wheel. I'm running unpinned versions. That's the one outstanding issue I'd fix next if you want it.

**Claude —** Done. The app is open in Chrome, signed in as **@jiayi.wu** (`jw5680@gsb.columbia.edu`).

## → http://localhost:3000 · API on http://localhost:8001

**Repo was already up to date** — `git fetch` returned nothing new. Local `main` matches `origin/main` at `b1dd1d2` (the merge of PR #9), and the working tree is clean. Nothing to pull.

## Ports

I surveyed every listener from 3000–10000 and identified each before touching anything, so I stopped only this app's:

| port | | action |
|---|---|---|
| 3000 | Next.js, this repo | stopped, restarted |
| 8001 | uvicorn, this repo | stopped, restarted |
| 8888 | **your JupyterLab** | left running |
| 5040 / 5678 / 5679 / 7260 / 7680 | Windows services, SecondScreen | left alone |
| 8000 | orphaned socket | **still stuck** |

**Port 8000 is still unusable.** I re-tested by attempting an actual bind — it fails. Those are the dead sockets left by the [unrelated project] processes from last session; the PIDs no longer exist, so nothing can be killed to reclaim them. **A reboot is the only fix.** Until then the API stays on 8001, with `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE` pointing there.

## One thing I had to fix

`/signin` was returning **404** on the first restart, even though `app/signin/page.tsx` exists and `/` served fine. Stale `.next` build cache — the dev server had been killed mid-write several times across sessions, leaving a corrupt route manifest. Deleting `.next` and restarting fixed it. Worth knowing: if routes 404 after an abrupt kill, that's the cause.

## Verified

All eight routes return 200 (`/`, `/signin`, `/signup`, `/sell`, `/saved`, `/inbox`, `/my-listings`, `/settings/profile`). API healthy at **v0.2.0**. Database intact from the last seed — 1,000 members, 1,350 listings, 5,399 photos — and since nothing in the repo changed, I skipped a reseed rather than spending the time.

Browser check passed clean: filter chips render the inherited profile default, removing one syncs the sidebar toggle, `Clear all` resets, radius tracks correctly (342 at 0.5 mi → 871 at 10 mi). No console or network errors.

Still outstanding, unchanged: `requirements.txt` won't install on Python 3.14 (`pandas==2.2.3`, `Pillow==11.1.0`, `pydantic-core` all fail to build). I'm running unpinned versions locally.

```
"use client";

import Link from "next/link";
import { usePathname, useRouter } from "next/navigation";
import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";

import { api } from "@/lib/api";
import type { Me } from "@/lib/types";
import { Logo } from "./Logo";
import { PinIcon, PlusIcon, SearchIcon } from "./ui";


/**
 * The avatar menu — UX_SPEC.md §6.6: "My listings / Saved items / Inbox /
 * Profile & account / Sign out".
 *
 * A menu rather than a straight link to the profile, because those three
 * collections have no other entry point on desktop; on mobile they are reached
 * from the tab bar instead.
 */
const MENU = [
  { href: "/my-listings", label: "My listings" },
  { href: "/saved", label: "Saved items" },
  { href: "/inbox", label: "Inbox" },
  { href: "/settings/profile", label: "Profile & account" },
];

function AvatarMenu({ me }: { me: Me | null }) {
  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
  const box = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const router = useRouter();

  // Close on an outside click or Escape — a menu you cannot dismiss with the
  // keyboard is a trap for anyone not using a mouse.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!open) return;
    const onClick = (e: MouseEvent) => {
      if (box.current && !box.current.contains(e.target as Node)) setOpen(false);
    };
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.key === "Escape") setOpen(false);
    };
    document.addEventListener("mousedown", onClick);
    document.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => {
      document.removeEventListener("mousedown", onClick);
      document.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    };
  }, [open]);

  async function signOut() {
    await api.signout().catch(() => undefined);
    router.push("/signin");
  }

  return (
    <div className="relative" ref={box}>
      <button
        type="button"
        onClick={() => setOpen((v) => !v)}
```

Four clicks into a settings page is the\n * wrong price for it.\n */\nfunction ZipPicker({ me, onChange }: { me: Me | null; onChange?: (me: Me) => void }) {\n  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);\n  const [q, setQ] = useState(\"\");\n  const [results, setResults] = useState<ZipResult[]>([]);\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\n  const [saving, setSaving] = useState(false);\n  const box = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!open) return;\n    const onClick = (e: MouseEvent) => {\n      if (box.current && !box.current.contains(e.target as Node)) setOpen(false);\n    };\n    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {\n      if (e.key === \"Escape\") setOpen(false);\n    };\n    document.addEventListener(\"mousedown\", onClick);\n    document.addEventListener(\"keydown\", onKey);\n    return () => {\n      document.removeEventListener(\"mousedown\", onClick);\n      document.removeEventListener(\"keydown\", onKey);\n    };\n  }, [open]);\n\n  // Same autocomplete the sign-up screen uses (§6.1): three digits is enough to\n  // narrow the metro list, and each row shows how far it is from campus.\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!open) return;\n    const t = setTimeout(() => {\n      api\n        .zips(q)\n        .then(setResults)\n        .catch(() => setResults([]));\n    }, 200);\n    return () => clearTimeout(t);\n  }, [q, open]);\n\n  async function choose(zip: string) {\n    setSaving(true);\n    setError(null);\n    try {\n      const updated = await api.updateMe({ zip_code: zip });\n      setOpen(false);\n      setQ(\"\");\n      // Distances and the facet

```json
{
  "file_path": "~\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\~\\ce1a8217-d177-4de2-93e2-e31db63a2e56\\scratchpad\\msg8.txt",
  "content": "Make controls look clickable, and let the ZIP chip change your ZIP\n\nTwo things reported from using the app.\n\nThe Reset button did not react to the mouse. It was not just Reset: Tailwind v4\nchanged Preflight to set `cursor: default` on buttons, and nothing in the app\ndeclares a cursor, so every button in the product looked inert -- the filter\nchips, the trust toggles, Load more, Email seller, Sign out. Restoring the\npointer once in globals.css fixes all of them, and covers select, summary and\nthe switch/checkbox roles the toggles use. Disabled controls get not-allowed.\nReset also had no hover state at all, so it gets one.\n\nThe ZIP chip in the top bar showed the ZIP and did nothing. It is now a button\nthat opens a picker: type three digits, get the metro list with neighbourhood\nand distance from campus, choose one. Profile & account keeps its ZIP field --\nthat is where you edit a profile deliberately -- but the ZIP is the origin every\ndistance on the feed is measured from, so trying a different one is a browsing\naction and should not cost four clicks into a settings page.\n\nChanging it PATCHes the profile, and the feed refetches rather than reloading,\nso the filters you had set survive the move. Pages that do not pass a handler\nfall back to a reload. The picker surfaces the API's own message when a ZIP is\noutside the New York metro, rather than failing silently.\n\nVerified in a browser: Reset, a trust switch and the sort select all report\ncursor=pointer; Reset changes colour and underlines on hover; the picker lists\nthe metro ZIPs with distances, and moving 10027 -> 10026 re-measured the feed\n(871 items within 10 miles of 10027 -> 872 of 10026) and persisted to GET /me.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\n"
}
```

> **Write**

```json
{
  "file_path": "~\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\~\\ce1a8217-d177-4de2-93e2-e31db63a2e56\\scratchpad\\pr-body2.md",
  "content": "Two things reported from using the app.\n\n## 1. Controls did not look clickable\n\nReset was the one noticed, but it was not a Reset problem. **Tailwind v4 changed Preflight to set `cursor: default` on buttons** (it was `pointer` in v3), and nothing in the app declares a cursor — so *every* button in the product looked inert on hover: the filter chips, the trust toggles, Load more, Email seller, Sign out, Clear all.\n\nFixed once in `globals.css` rather than per component, covering `select`, `summary`, and the `switch` / `checkbox` roles the toggles use. Disabled controls get `not-allowed`.\n\nReset also had no hover state at all, so it gained one.\n\n## 2. The ZIP chip showed the ZIP and did nothing\n\nIt is now a button that opens a picker: type three digits, get the metro list with neighbourhood and distance from campus, choose one.\n\nProfile & account keeps its ZIP field — that is where you edit a profile deliberately — but the ZIP is the origin **every distance on the feed is measured from**, so trying a different one is a browsing action. Four clicks into a settings page is the wrong price for it.\n\nDetails worth knowing:\n\n- Changing it `PATCH`es the profile, and the feed **refetches rather than reloading**, so the filters you had set survive the move. Pages that pass no handler fall back to a reload.\n- The picker surfaces the API's own message when a ZIP is outside the New York metro, instead of failing silently.\n- The current ZIP is highlighted in the list, and there is a link through to the full profile.\n\n## Verified in a browser\n\n| check | result |\n|---|---|\n| Reset / trust switch / sort select | all report `cursor: pointer` |\n| Reset on hover | colour shifts and underlines |\n| ZIP picker | lists metro ZIPs with neighbourhood + distance |\n| Moving 10027 → 10026 | headline went `871 items within 10 miles of 10027` → `872 ... of 10026` |\n| Persistence | `GET /me` returns the new `zip_code` |\n\n`tsc --noEmit` clean. No console or network errors.\n\n## Not included\n\nThe mobile header renders the ZIP as a plain heading; this change is desktop top-bar only. Worth a follow-up if the mobile layout wants the same affordance.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"
```

**Claude —** Both done, and **PR #10** is open: https://github.com/AnalyticsInPython/secondhand_marketplace/pull/10

## 1. The Reset button

It wasn't a Reset problem — **Tailwind v4 changed Preflight to set `cursor: default` on buttons** (it was `pointer` in v3). Nothing in the app declares a cursor, so *every* button looked inert on hover: the filter chips, trust toggles, Load more, Email seller, Sign out, Clear all.

I fixed it once in `globals.css` rather than patching Reset alone, since patching one button would have left the same bug everywhere else. It covers `select`, `summary`, and the `switch`/`checkbox` roles the toggles use; disabled controls get `not-allowed`.

Reset also had *no* hover state at all, so it now shifts colour and underlines.

Verified: Reset, a trust switch, and the sort dropdown all report `cursor: pointer`.

## 2. The ZIP chip

Now a button opening a picker — type three digits, get the metro list with neighbourhood and distance from campus, pick one. Current ZIP is highlighted, and there's a link through to the full profile.

Profile & account keeps its ZIP field, as you asked. The reasoning for having both: that page is where you edit a profile *deliberately*, but the ZIP is the origin every distance on the feed is measured from, so trying a different one is a browsing action.

Two things I decided while building it:

**It refetches instead of reloading.** A page reload would have been simpler but would discard whatever filters you'd set. Moving 10027 → 10026 re-measured the feed (`871 items within 10 miles of 10027` → `872 ... of 10026`) with filters intact, and persisted to `GET /me`.

**It surfaces the backend's own error.** `PATCH /me` rejects non-NYC ZIPs with a real message; the picker shows that rather than failing silently.

## One thing I left out

The **mobile** header renders the ZIP as a plain heading — this change is desktop top-bar only. I flagged it in the PR as a follow-up rather than widening the change without asking.

The dev servers are still running, so you can try both now at http://localhost:3000 — the CSS and the picker are already live via hot reload.

```
# Data & visualisation spec

**Owner:** Kobe · **Scope:** the behavioural ("user action") data and what can be
built on it · **Companion to:** [`mock_data_spec.md`](mock_data_spec.md), which
covers users, listings and photos.

> **If you are about to plot something from this data, read §6 first.** Several of
> the effects the charts will show were put there deliberately. Which ones, and
> how large, is written down — but a chart made without knowing that is a chart
> that claims more than it can.

---

## 1. What this covers

`mock_data_spec.md` describes the *content* of the marketplace — who the members
are and what they listed. This document describes what those members **did**:
every impression, save, contact, filter toggle, search and completed sale, and
```
</details>

**Claude —** Pulled. `main` is at **`f07830d`**, working tree clean.

## What landed

**PR #10 — yours, merged.** The cursor fix and the ZIP picker are on main now.

**PR #11 — "Record what members actually did"** (Kobe). This is a schema change, and a significant one:

| addition | why it matters |
|---|---|
| `search_events` table | new: query, result count, and which listing was clicked |
| `session_id` on views, saves, enquiries, filter events | ties a member's actions into one visit |
| `listings.buyer_id` + `sold_price_cents` | who actually bought it, and for how much — not just the asking price |
| `listing_views.badges_shown` | **the badge experiment coin flip is now in the data** |
| `docs/data_visualization_spec.md` | 304 lines, Kobe's spec for what can be plotted |

Two of these are worth calling out.

**The badge experiment is back.** `badges_shown` per impression is exactly the randomisation the LionsList spec called "the one genuinely causal result" and that `UX_SPEC.md` had dropped. When I flagged its absence a few days ago, this closes it — the data now has two arms.

**`sold_price_cents` separates asking price from sale price.** That makes the in-group discount question in `PROPOSAL.md` answerable for the first time; until now every price in the corpus was an asking price.

Also worth reading before anyone plots anything — the new doc opens with a warning:

> *Several of the effects the charts will show were put there deliberately. Which ones, and how large, is written down — but a chart made without knowing that is a chart that claims more than it can.*

## State of your running app

I only pulled, as asked — I haven't restarted anything. But be aware:

- **The database is now stale.** New tables and columns exist in the code that `columbia_market.db` doesn't have. The API on 8001 is still serving the *old* code from memory (no `--reload`), so it works for the moment.
- **The frontend will hot-reload** the new `listings/[id]` changes, which expect API fields the running backend doesn't return yet. Expect the item detail page to misbehave until the backend restarts.

Want me to reseed and restart both? That's `python -m scripts.seed --reset` plus a bounce of the two servers — the data files were fully regenerated in this PR, so it's a real reseed rather than a no-op.

**Claude —** Reseeded and both restarted, open in Chrome signed in as **@ekaterina_ko**.

## → http://localhost:3000 · API on http://localhost:8001

`--reset` drops and recreates every table, so the new schema came in cleanly — no need to delete the database file. No dependency changes in PR #11, so no reinstall.

## PR #11's data verified

**The badge experiment has two real arms** — this is the headline:

```
badges_shown = False    32,044 impressions
badges_shown = True     31,976 impressions
```

That's the randomisation the LionsList spec called "the one genuinely causal result," which `UX_SPEC.md` had dropped and I flagged as missing. It's now in the corpus, split almost exactly 50/50.

**Sale prices are distinct from asking prices**, which makes the in-group discount question answerable for the first time:

```
452 sold listings · 452 with a sale price · 323 with a known buyer
mean asking $81.12  vs  mean sale $79.47
```

The 129 sales with no buyer are deliberate per the loader's comment — someone who bought without ever enquiring, which is a real and measurable case rather than missing data.

**Sessions tie actions together** — all 77,107 event rows across five tables carry a `session_id`, spanning 17,141 distinct sessions. **Searches** are new: 2,774 events, 40 distinct queries, 21.7% click-through, led by *vacuum*, *rice cooker*, *desk*.

## App verified

All seven routes 200, API healthy at v0.2.0. The UI checks still pass on the new data: cursors are `pointer` on buttons, switches and the sort select; Reset changes on hover; the ZIP picker moved 10027 → 10026, re-measured the feed, and persisted. No console or network errors.

One note: the feed total dropped slightly (659 → 653 within 2.5 mi) because the corpus was fully regenerated in this PR, not just extended — the listings themselves are different rows now.

## What landed: PR #12, the dashboard

A new `/insights` page (917 lines) plus `backend/app/routers/insights.py` (676 lines). **No schema or data change**, so no reseed was needed — but the backend runs without `--reload`, so I restarted it to pick up the new router.

**My ZIP picker survived the merge intact**, and PR #12 added a *Dashboard* link beside it in the top bar, with a comment explaining the placement: *"nobody finds an analytics page hidden behind an avatar."*

## It works, and it's good

Topline for the last complete week, each with a week-over-week delta:

```
ITEMS LISTED 53 ▲8.2%    VALUE SOLD $1,678 ▲86.4%    NEW MEMBERS 54
ITEMS SOLD   21 ▲31.2%   SELL-THROUGH 39.62% ▲21.3%  ACTIVE 544 ▲24.5%
CONTACTS     34 ▼17.1%   SEARCHES 103 ▲119.1%
```

Below that: listed-vs-sold by week, category breakdown, a price-by-condition chart with interquartile bars, inventory age, and search analysis. Nine inline SVG charts, no chart library.

Two findings on the page are genuinely sharp — it isn't just plotting numbers:

**68.3% of live inventory is over three months old.** The page says why that matters: *"A feed padded with stale listings looks fuller than it is — those items are scrolled past, not considered."*

**32.7% of searches return nothing**, and it diagnoses the cause rather than reporting the number: *"We have plenty of textbooks and desk chairs — but their titles say 'Corporate Finance (Berk) 5th ed.' and 'HON Ignition, size C', never the words someone would type. Search matches on the title only, so the catalogue is there and unreachable."*

That last one is a real product bug surfaced by the data — search is title-only, so `textbook` (82 searches), `office chair` (79) and `bookshelf` (76) all return zero against a catalogue that has those items.

## Verified

`tsc --noEmit` clean, `/insights` returns 200, API healthy at v0.2.0, 279 lines of content rendered, **no console or network errors**. The feed, ZIP picker and cursor fixes all still work.

Both servers are up — http://localhost:3000, API on 8001. The dashboard is reachable from the top bar.
